Not really. I mean, I try the old things—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with some little electronics project—but most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there.

Now I’ll start doing it and then realize I’m just standing there, not really getting anything from it. Now most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still do them sometimes, but it’s more like… I’m going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of sadness been constant for the past year or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much there, yeah. I mean, not exactly every minute in the same way, but I’d say it started around a year and two months ago.\n\nThat was when the pipe burst in my old place and I had to move into this smaller apartment, and all my routines kind of fell apart. It sounds stupid, because it’s just an apartment, but I had things set up a certain way, you know? After that, I don’t think I really got back to feeling normal. There might be little moments where it’s less noticeable, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a real stretch where I felt better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods over the past year where your sadness felt significantly better for a while before returning?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe a day here or there where I was distracted, but not a real stretch where I’d say it got significantly better.\n\nIt’s been going on about a year and two months now, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. After that, all my routines kind of fell apart. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up. Like at work I’ve been falling behind, and then with my apartment, things got away from me too, and it all sort of piles up in my head.\n\nIt’s like when one cable is bad in a whole setup—sorry, I do computer support, so I think in those terms—and then suddenly everything downstream is failing and it’s probably because I didn’t catch it soon enough. That’s how it feels with me. Maybe a little here and there, but not in a way that feels like relief, if that makes sense. Most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy sadness.

Sometimes I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of change the room for me, or I’ll try cooking something with enough steps that I have to pay attention. I used to tinker with electronics too, little parts spread out on the table, and that would reset me somehow. Now it’s more like I’m doing those things and they’re happening in front of me, but they don’t really reach me.

So… slightly less heavy, maybe for a few minutes if I’m distracted, but it’s still mostly there.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s… I don’t know if it’s worse exactly, but it feels more settled in now. At first I kept thinking I could shake it off if I got back into the right routine, you know, put on a record, make something decent to eat, fix some little gadget or whatever.

But now most days I wake up and it’s just already there, that flat, heavy sadness. Those things still happen sometimes—I’ll put the needle down on a record and then start thinking about how I used to organize them by the mood of the album, which is probably ridiculous—but they don’t really reach me anymore. It’s like the sadness is just sitting over everything.

It started after that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. Everything got thrown off—my routines, where I kept things, even stupid stuff like where I’d make coffee—and I know that sounds minor, but once that happened it just… never really went back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to enjoy the things you used to like doing, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really… maybe for a minute here or there, but it doesn’t stick. Like I’m doing the motions, but the signal’s not getting through.\n\nDoctor: Do you find any pleasure in social interactions with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Friends or family… yeah, I mean, I still see them sometimes, but it’s not like it used to be. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think… yes, in a way. Not like one big drop-off, but it’s gotten more fixed, more there when I wake up.

Most days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already sitting on me. And the things that used to kind of reset me—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with electronics—those don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still try them sometimes. I had this old receiver I kept meaning to open up, actually, and I used to like figuring out what was wrong with things like that… but now it’s like my hands are doing it and the rest of me isn’t really there.

So, yeah. Maybe worse, or maybe just heavier because it doesn’t lift much.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up. Like at work I’ve been falling behind, and then with my apartment, things got away from me too, and it all sort of piles up in my head.\n\nIt’s like when one cable is bad in a whole setup—sorry, I do computer support, so I think in those terms—and then suddenly everything downstream is failing and it’s probably because I didn’t catch it soon enough. That’s how it feels with me. Like I’ve ruined things for people, and I deserve whatever bad thing comes next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I wake up and it’s just this flat, heavy kind of sadness sitting there before I even do anything.\n\nIt’s weird, because I used to have little things that kind of reset me—putting on a record, making something decent to eat, messing around with old electronics or fixing some little board or cable—and lately it’s like none of that reaches me. Like the signal doesn’t get through. So… yeah, sad or empty, that’s pretty much most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and a couple months, I think. It started after that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. Everything got thrown off—my routines, where I kept things, even stupid stuff like where I’d make coffee—and I know that sounds minor, but once that happened it just… never really went back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to enjoy the things you used to like doing, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really… maybe for a minute here or there, but it doesn’t stick. I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of straighten me out, you know, like the room would feel different after a few songs. Or I’ll try cooking something, or pick up some little electronics thing I meant to fix.\n\nBut most days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just… don’t reach me anymore. Like I’m doing the motions, but the signal’s not getting through.\n\nDoctor: Do you find any pleasure in social interactions with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Friends or family… yeah, I mean, I still see them sometimes, but it’s not like it used to be. I’ll be sitting there and they’re talking, and part of me knows I should feel something from it, like comfort or whatever, but most days I’ve already got that flat, heavy sadness sitting on me.\n\nIt’s kind of like when I put on a record or try to cook something decent, or mess with some little electronics thing—those used to sort of reset me. Same with people, I guess. Now it just doesn’t really reach me the way it should.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I try the old things—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with some little electronics project—but most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there.\n\nIt’s like… those things used to reset me somehow. Like if the turntable was working right, or I got a sauce to come out okay, I’d feel a little more like myself. Now I’ll start doing it and then realize I’m just standing there, not really getting anything from it. It doesn’t reach me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep trying to go back to the things that used to help—like putting on a record, making something to eat, messing with some little electronics part on the table.\n\nI used to get something from that, like it would sort of reset me. Now most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still do them sometimes, but it’s more like… I’m going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of sadness been constant for the past year or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much there, yeah. I mean, not exactly every minute in the same way, but I’d say it started around a year and two months ago.\n\nThat was when the pipe burst in my old place and I had to move into this smaller apartment, and all my routines kind of fell apart. It sounds stupid, because it’s just an apartment, but I had things set up a certain way, you know? After that, I don’t think I really got back to feeling normal. There might be little moments where it’s less noticeable, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a real stretch where I felt better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods over the past year where your sadness felt significantly better for a while before returning?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe a day here or there where I was distracted, but not a real stretch where I’d say it got significantly better.\n\nIt’s been going on about a year and two months now, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. After that, all my routines kind of fell apart. I had this whole setup before—where things went, when I’d do stuff—and once that was gone, I don’t know… it just never really lifted for any length of time.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt any improvement in your sadness over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. I keep thinking maybe if I just put on the right record or cook something decent, it’ll click back into place, but most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy kind of sadness.\n\nIt’s funny, I used to fix little electronics things on the table and it would calm me down, like troubleshooting at work but without people waiting on me. Now I’ll sit there with the parts out and just… nothing really reaches me. So over the past few weeks, I wouldn’t say it’s improved much.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness usually present for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, it’s there for a lot of the day. I usually wake up and it’s already kind of sitting on me, that flat, heavy feeling.\n\nI’ll try to do things that used to sort of reset me, like put on a record or cook something, or mess with little electronics parts at the table, but it’s like they don’t really get through anymore. Like I’m doing the motions, but the sadness is still there in the background… or not even the background, really. More like the room I’m in.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods over the past year where you felt significantly better, even if it was just for a short time?\n\nPatient: No, not a distinct period, not really. I mean… this whole thing’s been going on about a year and two months, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment.\n\nIt sounds silly when I say it like that, like an apartment should just be an apartment, but my routines were all tied to the old place—where things were, how I’d move through the day. After that, everything kind of fell apart, and I don’t remember any real stretch where I felt significantly better. Maybe a little distracted here and there, but not better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times when your sadness feels slightly less heavy than at other times?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not in a way that feels like relief, if that makes sense. Most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy sadness.\n\nSometimes I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of change the room for me, or I’ll try cooking something with enough steps that I have to pay attention. I used to tinker with electronics too, little parts spread out on the table, and that would reset me somehow. Now it’s more like I’m doing those things and they’re happening in front of me, but they don’t really reach me.\n\nSo… slightly less heavy, maybe for a few minutes if I’m distracted, but it’s still mostly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difference in how bad your sadness feels now compared to when it first started?\n\nPatient: It’s… I don’t know if it’s worse exactly, but it feels more settled in now. At first I kept thinking I could shake it off if I got back into the right routine, you know, put on a record, make something decent to eat, fix some little gadget or whatever.\n\nBut now most days I wake up and it’s just already there, that flat, heavy sadness. Those things still happen sometimes—I’ll put the needle down on a record and then start thinking about how I used to organize them by the mood of the album, which is probably ridiculous—but they don’t really reach me anymore. It’s like the sadness is just sitting over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the sadness has gotten worse over the past year?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a way. Not like one big drop-off, but it’s gotten more fixed, more there when I wake up.\n\nMost days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already sitting on me. So… yeah, sad or empty, that’s pretty much most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and a couple months, I think. It started after that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. Everything got thrown off—my routines, where I kept things, even stupid stuff like where I’d make coffee—and I know that sounds minor, but once that happened it just… never really went back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to enjoy the things you used to like doing, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really… maybe for a minute here or there, but it doesn’t stick. I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of straighten me out, you know, like the room would feel different after a few songs. Or I’ll try cooking something, or pick up some little electronics thing I meant to fix.\n\nBut most days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just… don’t reach me anymore. Like I’m doing the motions, but the signal’s not getting through.\n\nDoctor: Do you find any pleasure in social interactions with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Friends or family… yeah, I mean, I still see them sometimes, but it’s not like it used to be. I’ll be sitting there and they’re talking, and part of me knows I should feel something from it, like comfort or whatever, but most days I’ve already got that flat, heavy sadness sitting on me.\n\nIt’s kind of like when I put on a record or try to cook something decent, or mess with some little electronics thing—those used to sort of reset me. Same with people, I guess. Now it just doesn’t really reach me the way it should.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I try the old things—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with some little electronics project—but most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there.\n\nIt’s like… those things used to reset me somehow. Like if the turntable was working right, or I got a sauce to come out okay, I’d feel a little more like myself. Now I’ll start doing it and then realize I’m just standing there, not really getting anything from it. It doesn’t reach me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep trying to go back to the things that used to help—like putting on a record, making something to eat, messing with some little electronics part on the table.\n\nI used to get something from that, like it would sort of reset me. Now most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still do them sometimes, but it’s more like… I’m going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of sadness been constant for the past year or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much there, yeah. I mean, not exactly every minute in the same way, but I’d say it started around a year and two months ago.\n\nThat was when the pipe burst in my old place and I had to move into this smaller apartment, and all my routines kind of fell apart. It sounds stupid, because it’s just an apartment, but I had things set up a certain way, you know? After that, I don’t think I really got back to feeling normal. There might be little moments where it’s less noticeable, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a real stretch where I felt better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods over the past year where your sadness felt significantly better for a while before returning?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe a day here or there where I was distracted, but not a real stretch where I’d say it got significantly better.\n\nIt’s been going on about a year and two months now, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. After that, all my routines kind of fell apart. I had this whole setup before—where things went, when I’d do stuff—and once that was gone, I don’t know… it just never really lifted for any length of time.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt any improvement in your sadness over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. I keep thinking maybe if I just put on the right record or cook something decent, it’ll click back into place, but most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy kind of sadness.\n\nIt’s funny, I used to fix little electronics things on the table and it would calm me down, like troubleshooting at work but without people waiting on me. Now I’ll sit there with the parts out and just… nothing really reaches me. So over the past few weeks, I wouldn’t say it’s improved much.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness usually present for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, it’s there for a lot of the day. I usually wake up and it’s already kind of sitting on me, that flat, heavy feeling.\n\nI’ll try to do things that used to sort of reset me, like put on a record or cook something, or mess with little electronics parts at the table, but it’s like they don’t really get through anymore. Like I’m doing the motions, but the sadness is still there in the background… or not even the background, really. More like the room I’m in.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods over the past year where you felt significantly better, even if it was just for a short time?\n\nPatient: No, not a distinct period, not really. I mean… this whole thing’s been going on about a year and two months, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment.\n\nIt sounds silly when I say it like that, like an apartment should just be an apartment, but my routines were all tied to the old place—where things were, how I’d move through the day. After that, everything kind of fell apart, and I don’t remember any real stretch where I felt significantly better. Maybe a little distracted here and there, but not better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times when your sadness feels slightly less heavy than at other times?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not in a way that feels like relief, if that makes sense. Most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy sadness.\n\nSometimes I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of change the room for me, or I’ll try cooking something with enough steps that I have to pay attention. I used to tinker with electronics too, little parts spread out on the table, and that would reset me somehow. Now it’s more like I’m doing those things and they’re happening in front of me, but they don’t really reach me.\n\nSo… slightly less heavy, maybe for a few minutes if I’m distracted, but it’s still mostly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difference in how bad your sadness feels now compared to when it first started?\n\nPatient: It’s… I don’t know if it’s worse exactly, but it feels more settled in now. At first I kept thinking I could shake it off if I got back into the right routine, you know, put on a record, make something decent to eat, fix some little gadget or whatever.\n\nBut now most days I wake up and it’s just already there, that flat, heavy sadness. Those things still happen sometimes—I’ll put the needle down on a record and then start thinking about how I used to organize them by the mood of the album, which is probably ridiculous—but they don’t really reach me anymore. It’s like the sadness is just sitting over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the sadness has gotten worse over the past year?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a way. Not like one big drop-off, but it’s gotten more fixed, more there when I wake up.\n\nMost days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already sitting on me. And the things that used to kind of reset me—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with electronics—those don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still try them sometimes. I had this old receiver I kept meaning to open up, actually, and I used to like figuring out what was wrong with things like that… but now it’s like my hands are doing it and the rest of me isn’t really there.\n\nSo, yeah. Maybe worse, or maybe just heavier because it doesn’t lift much.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel this sad for a few hours, or does it last all day?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly all day, I think. I mean, there might be little stretches where I’m distracted, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, this flat, heavy sadness.\n\nI used to be able to shift it a little by putting on a record or cooking something, or messing around with electronics—like taking something apart and figuring it out—but lately those things just don’t really reach me. It’s like the sadness is just sitting over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the sadness has gotten worse over the past year?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a way. Maybe worse, or maybe just heavier because it doesn’t lift much.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel this sad for a few hours, or does it last all day?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly all day, I think. Like I’ve ruined things for people, and I deserve whatever bad thing comes next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I wake up and it’s just this flat, heavy kind of sadness sitting there before I even do anything.\n\nIt’s weird, because I used to have little things that kind of reset me—putting on a record, making something decent to eat, messing around with old electronics or fixing some little board or cable—and lately it’s like none of that reaches me. Like the signal doesn’t get through. So… yeah, sad or empty, that’s pretty much most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and a couple months, I think. It started after that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. Everything got thrown off—my routines, where I kept things, even stupid stuff like where I’d make coffee—and I know that sounds minor, but once that happened it just… never really went back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to enjoy the things you used to like doing, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really… maybe for a minute here or there, but it doesn’t stick. I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of straighten me out, you know, like the room would feel different after a few songs. Or I’ll try cooking something, or pick up some little electronics thing I meant to fix.\n\nBut most days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just… don’t reach me anymore. Like I’m doing the motions, but the signal’s not getting through.\n\nDoctor: Do you find any pleasure in social interactions with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Friends or family… yeah, I mean, I still see them sometimes, but it’s not like it used to be. I’ll be sitting there and they’re talking, and part of me knows I should feel something from it, like comfort or whatever, but most days I’ve already got that flat, heavy sadness sitting on me.\n\nIt’s kind of like when I put on a record or try to cook something decent, or mess with some little electronics thing—those used to sort of reset me. Same with people, I guess. Now it just doesn’t really reach me the way it should.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I try the old things—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with some little electronics project—but most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there.\n\nIt’s like… those things used to reset me somehow. Like if the turntable was working right, or I got a sauce to come out okay, I’d feel a little more like myself. Now I’ll start doing it and then realize I’m just standing there, not really getting anything from it. It doesn’t reach me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep trying to go back to the things that used to help—like putting on a record, making something to eat, messing with some little electronics part on the table.\n\nI used to get something from that, like it would sort of reset me. Now most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still do them sometimes, but it’s more like… I’m going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of sadness been constant for the past year or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much there, yeah. I mean, not exactly every minute in the same way, but I’d say it started around a year and two months ago.\n\nThat was when the pipe burst in my old place and I had to move into this smaller apartment, and all my routines kind of fell apart. It sounds stupid, because it’s just an apartment, but I had things set up a certain way, you know? After that, I don’t think I really got back to feeling normal. There might be little moments where it’s less noticeable, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a real stretch where I felt better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods over the past year where your sadness felt significantly better for a while before returning?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe a day here or there where I was distracted, but not a real stretch where I’d say it got significantly better.\n\nIt’s been going on about a year and two months now, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. After that, all my routines kind of fell apart. I had this whole setup before—where things went, when I’d do stuff—and once that was gone, I don’t know… it just never really lifted for any length of time.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt any improvement in your sadness over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. I keep thinking maybe if I just put on the right record or cook something decent, it’ll click back into place, but most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy kind of sadness.\n\nIt’s funny, I used to fix little electronics things on the table and it would calm me down, like troubleshooting at work but without people waiting on me. Now I’ll sit there with the parts out and just… nothing really reaches me. So over the past few weeks, I wouldn’t say it’s improved much.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness usually present for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, it’s there for a lot of the day. I usually wake up and it’s already kind of sitting on me, that flat, heavy feeling.\n\nI’ll try to do things that used to sort of reset me, like put on a record or cook something, or mess with little electronics parts at the table, but it’s like they don’t really get through anymore. Like I’m doing the motions, but the sadness is still there in the background… or not even the background, really. More like the room I’m in.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods over the past year where you felt significantly better, even if it was just for a short time?\n\nPatient: No, not a distinct period, not really. I mean… this whole thing’s been going on about a year and two months, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment.\n\nIt sounds silly when I say it like that, like an apartment should just be an apartment, but my routines were all tied to the old place—where things were, how I’d move through the day. After that, everything kind of fell apart, and I don’t remember any real stretch where I felt significantly better. Maybe a little distracted here and there, but not better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times when your sadness feels slightly less heavy than at other times?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not in a way that feels like relief, if that makes sense. Most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy sadness.\n\nSometimes I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of change the room for me, or I’ll try cooking something with enough steps that I have to pay attention. I used to tinker with electronics too, little parts spread out on the table, and that would reset me somehow. Now it’s more like I’m doing those things and they’re happening in front of me, but they don’t really reach me.\n\nSo… slightly less heavy, maybe for a few minutes if I’m distracted, but it’s still mostly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difference in how bad your sadness feels now compared to when it first started?\n\nPatient: It’s… I don’t know if it’s worse exactly, but it feels more settled in now. At first I kept thinking I could shake it off if I got back into the right routine, you know, put on a record, make something decent to eat, fix some little gadget or whatever.\n\nBut now most days I wake up and it’s just already there, that flat, heavy sadness. Those things still happen sometimes—I’ll put the needle down on a record and then start thinking about how I used to organize them by the mood of the album, which is probably ridiculous—but they don’t really reach me anymore. It’s like the sadness is just sitting over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the sadness has gotten worse over the past year?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a way. Not like one big drop-off, but it’s gotten more fixed, more there when I wake up.\n\nMost days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already sitting on me. And the things that used to kind of reset me—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with electronics—those don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still try them sometimes. I had this old receiver I kept meaning to open up, actually, and I used to like figuring out what was wrong with things like that… but now it’s like my hands are doing it and the rest of me isn’t really there.\n\nSo, yeah. Maybe worse, or maybe just heavier because it doesn’t lift much.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel this sad for a few hours, or does it last all day?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly all day, I think. I mean, there might be little stretches where I’m distracted, but most days I wake up and it’s already there, this flat, heavy sadness.\n\nI used to be able to shift it a little by putting on a record or cooking something, or messing around with electronics—like taking something apart and figuring it out—but lately those things just don’t really reach me. So it’s not just a few hours and then it passes. It kind of sits there through the day.\n\nDoctor: Has the intensity of your sadness changed significantly at any point over the past year?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there was one point where it suddenly got much worse. It’s more like… over time it got harder to shake.\n\nMost days now I wake up and it’s already there, that flat, heavy sadness. And the things that used to sort of reset me—putting on a record, cooking something, taking apart some little electronic thing just to see if I could fix it—they don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still do them sometimes, or think about doing them, and then I get sidetracked by, like, where I put the tiny screwdrivers or whether I should reorganize the records again… but the sadness just stays there underneath it.\n\nDoctor: Has your sadness felt any different to you over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the past couple weeks it’s been pretty much the same, maybe just more noticeable because I keep waking up and it’s already there. That flat, heavy sadness.\n\nIt’s strange, because I’ll try to do the little things that used to help—put on a record, make something to eat, mess with some old piece of electronics—but it’s like they don’t land anymore. I was looking at this receiver the other day, thinking I should open it up, and then I just… didn’t really care. So, different? I don’t know. Mostly it just feels like it’s been sitting on me most days.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I can’t shake this feeling that I’ve messed everything up. Like at work I’ve been falling behind, and then with my apartment, things got away from me too, and it all sort of piles up in my head.\n\nIt’s like when one cable is bad in a whole setup—sorry, I do computer support, so I think in those terms—and then suddenly everything downstream is failing and it’s probably because I didn’t catch it soon enough. That’s how it feels with me. Like I’ve ruined things for people, and I deserve whatever bad thing comes next.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think. I wake up and it’s just this flat, heavy kind of sadness sitting there before I even do anything.\n\nIt’s weird, because I used to have little things that kind of reset me—putting on a record, making something decent to eat, messing around with old electronics or fixing some little board or cable—and lately it’s like none of that reaches me. Like the signal doesn’t get through. So… yeah, sad or empty, that’s pretty much most of the time.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About… a year and a couple months, I think. It started after that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. Everything got thrown off—my routines, where I kept things, even stupid stuff like where I’d make coffee—and I know that sounds minor, but once that happened it just… never really went back to normal.\n\nDoctor: Are you still able to enjoy the things you used to like doing, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really… maybe for a minute here or there, but it doesn’t stick. I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of straighten me out, you know, like the room would feel different after a few songs. Or I’ll try cooking something, or pick up some little electronics thing I meant to fix.\n\nBut most days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just… don’t reach me anymore. Like I’m doing the motions, but the signal’s not getting through.\n\nDoctor: Do you find any pleasure in social interactions with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Friends or family… yeah, I mean, I still see them sometimes, but it’s not like it used to be. I’ll be sitting there and they’re talking, and part of me knows I should feel something from it, like comfort or whatever, but most days I’ve already got that flat, heavy sadness sitting on me.\n\nIt’s kind of like when I put on a record or try to cook something decent, or mess with some little electronics thing—those used to sort of reset me. Same with people, I guess. Now it just doesn’t really reach me the way it should.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I try the old things—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with some little electronics project—but most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there.\n\nIt’s like… those things used to reset me somehow. Like if the turntable was working right, or I got a sauce to come out okay, I’d feel a little more like myself. Now I’ll start doing it and then realize I’m just standing there, not really getting anything from it. It doesn’t reach me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I keep trying to go back to the things that used to help—like putting on a record, making something to eat, messing with some little electronics part on the table.\n\nI used to get something from that, like it would sort of reset me. Now most days I wake up and there’s this flat, heavy sadness already there, and those things just don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still do them sometimes, but it’s more like… I’m going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of sadness been constant for the past year or have you had periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much there, yeah. I mean, not exactly every minute in the same way, but I’d say it started around a year and two months ago.\n\nThat was when the pipe burst in my old place and I had to move into this smaller apartment, and all my routines kind of fell apart. It sounds stupid, because it’s just an apartment, but I had things set up a certain way, you know? After that, I don’t think I really got back to feeling normal. There might be little moments where it’s less noticeable, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a real stretch where I felt better.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods over the past year where your sadness felt significantly better for a while before returning?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe a day here or there where I was distracted, but not a real stretch where I’d say it got significantly better.\n\nIt’s been going on about a year and two months now, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment. After that, all my routines kind of fell apart. I had this whole setup before—where things went, when I’d do stuff—and once that was gone, I don’t know… it just never really lifted for any length of time.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt any improvement in your sadness over the past few weeks?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. I keep thinking maybe if I just put on the right record or cook something decent, it’ll click back into place, but most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy kind of sadness.\n\nIt’s funny, I used to fix little electronics things on the table and it would calm me down, like troubleshooting at work but without people waiting on me. Now I’ll sit there with the parts out and just… nothing really reaches me. So over the past few weeks, I wouldn’t say it’s improved much.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of sadness usually present for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, it’s there for a lot of the day. I usually wake up and it’s already kind of sitting on me, that flat, heavy feeling.\n\nI’ll try to do things that used to sort of reset me, like put on a record or cook something, or mess with little electronics parts at the table, but it’s like they don’t really get through anymore. Like I’m doing the motions, but the sadness is still there in the background… or not even the background, really. More like the room I’m in.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods over the past year where you felt significantly better, even if it was just for a short time?\n\nPatient: No, not a distinct period, not really. I mean… this whole thing’s been going on about a year and two months, since that pipe burst and I had to move into the smaller apartment.\n\nIt sounds silly when I say it like that, like an apartment should just be an apartment, but my routines were all tied to the old place—where things were, how I’d move through the day. After that, everything kind of fell apart, and I don’t remember any real stretch where I felt significantly better. Maybe a little distracted here and there, but not better.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times when your sadness feels slightly less heavy than at other times?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little here and there, but not in a way that feels like relief, if that makes sense. Most days I wake up and it’s already there—that flat, heavy sadness.\n\nSometimes I’ll put on a record because that used to kind of change the room for me, or I’ll try cooking something with enough steps that I have to pay attention. I used to tinker with electronics too, little parts spread out on the table, and that would reset me somehow. Now it’s more like I’m doing those things and they’re happening in front of me, but they don’t really reach me.\n\nSo… slightly less heavy, maybe for a few minutes if I’m distracted, but it’s still mostly there.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difference in how bad your sadness feels now compared to when it first started?\n\nPatient: It’s… I don’t know if it’s worse exactly, but it feels more settled in now. At first I kept thinking I could shake it off if I got back into the right routine, you know, put on a record, make something decent to eat, fix some little gadget or whatever.\n\nBut now most days I wake up and it’s just already there, that flat, heavy sadness. Those things still happen sometimes—I’ll put the needle down on a record and then start thinking about how I used to organize them by the mood of the album, which is probably ridiculous—but they don’t really reach me anymore. It’s like the sadness is just sitting over everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the sadness has gotten worse over the past year?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in a way. Not like one big drop-off, but it’s gotten more fixed, more there when I wake up.\n\nMost days I wake up with this flat, heavy sadness already sitting on me. And the things that used to kind of reset me—putting on a record, cooking something, tinkering with electronics—those don’t really reach me anymore. I’ll still try them sometimes. I had this old receiver I kept meaning to open up, actually, and I used to like figuring out what was wrong with things like that… but now it’s like my hands are doing it and the rest of me isn’t really there.\n\nSo, yeah. Maybe worse, or maybe just heavier because it doesn’t lift much.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel this sad for a few hours, or does it last all day?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly all day, I think.
